Abstract: Dilution of precision is the standard way to describe navigation geometry and it does not apply in cislunar space: its derivation presumes a snapshot solution in which simultaneous measurements determine the state at one instant. A spacecraft tracked by inter-satellite ranging in the Earth-Moon three-body problem has no such solution: range is a function of position only, so the velocity columns of the measurement Jacobian vanish identically and the state is unobservable however many links are in view. The correct figure of merit is dynamical, because the three-body flow carries velocity information into later range measurements. We demonstrate this on four differential-corrected planar distant retrograde orbits, reporting the observability Gramian rather than a dilution of precision. Instantaneous range-only geometry has rank 2 of 4; the full state becomes observable only after a tracking arc of 2.09 h, the first sampled arc at full rank on a 0.26 h grid, and a square-root information filter recomputing the same quantity by a different route becomes well-posed at exactly that arc. Beyond it, conditioning improves monotonically with arc length, the Gramian condition number falling from 1.2e11 at 3 h to 1.4e5 at 48 h. Adding range rate lifts the instantaneous rank from 2 to 4, so a Doppler-capable link makes a snapshot solution exist where a range-only link cannot. Lifting the known-reference assumption, the joint problem of estimating a station and the satellites' states and clocks together is rank-deficient by exactly one without an external interferometric observable in all twenty realizations tested, and full rank with it; the deficiency persists only to four satellites. For the configurations studied, cislunar navigation requirements are better written as arc-length, conditioning and measurement-type budgets than as dilution-of-precision thresholds, which are undefined here.

</details>

Abstract: This paper presents sequence-to-sequence (seq2seq) neural surrogates for high-speed transient response modeling and signal integrity analysis in fixed and variable design parameter spaces. To identify the structural requirements for prediction accuracy, multiple neural architectures were evaluated with respect to different training strategies such as sample-based and unit interval-based, as well as input configurations such as TX-only and TX+RX. For fixed design parameters, feedforward neural networks achieve accuracy comparable to recurrent models while requiring lower computational cost, as the static mapping can be approximated by fully connected and non-sequential representations. Variable design parameters alter the electromagnetic characteristics of the interconnect, which creates parameter-dependent temporal dynamics that require sequence-aware architectures to resolve the evolving interactions within the transient response. The results based on a 14-dimensional variable design parameter scenario show that while autoregressive TX+RX feedback suffers from severe phase drift and error propagation under variable conditions, non-autoregressive TX-only inference leads to stability. To overcome the structural limitations of standard decoders, a recurrent Early Fusion LSTM is proposed, which injects design parameters at the initial encoding stage. This approach achieves superior prediction accuracy, with an RMSE of 31.0 mV and an R2 of 99.51% for transient waveform prediction, an RMSE of 30.7 mV and an R2 of 99.57% for eye-height prediction, and an RMSE of 19.5 ps and an R2 of 99.94% for eye-width prediction. These results demonstrate that neural seq2seq surrogates are accurate for transient response modeling and signal integrity analysis in complex variable design parameter spaces.

</details>

Abstract: Recent advances in chemical computing create new opportunities to realize biocompatible communication devices for application in precision medicine. Implanted microscopic transmitters and receivers can exchange information using molecules that diffuse through a biological medium, enabling them to form a network and jointly coordinate sensing and actuation operations. However, randomness in molecular propagation causes severe signal distortion, and receivers have limited computational capacity to compensate. The receiver design must therefore combine robustness to diffusion-induced distortion with computational architectures amenable to implementation in the chemical domain. To meet these requirements, we propose a physics-informed reinforcement learning (RL) framework implemented with the reservoir computing (RC) architecture. We study the task of synchronizing the receiver's clock with a sequence transmitted through a molecular communication (MC) channel. The physics part approximates the maximum-likelihood function synchronization objective, while the other learns a timing policy using this objective as its reward. Simulation results yield clock misalignments above half a second, compatible with molecular transmissions operating on the timescale of seconds.

</details>

Abstract: Illicit activity in financial networks is difficult to detect. We exploit the idea that the chance of a transaction being illicit depends partly on the concurrent positions and recent actions of counterparties, as well as the higher-order organization of fund flows. We combine hypergraph features and machine learning to demonstrate practical ef fectiveness. Specifically, we use the Elliptic++ Bitcoin dataset to show that graph and directed-hypergraph-based features improve illicit-address classification. The suc cess of the predictive model then allows us to examine potential money-laundering chains are structurally fragile. Several illicit-to-licit transaction motifs with unknown intermediary addresses specified, and the fragility of these motifs is evaluated through progressive removal of the highest-risk intermediaries. Surprisingly, these motifs are fragile, suggesting that money laundering could be disrupted via the identification and exclusion of owners of a limited number of crypto wallets.

</details>

Abstract: High-frequency (HF) skywave radars require high gain at elevation angles of 3 degrees to 30 degrees, where vertically-polarized monopoles over lossy land suffer from pseudo-Brewster cancellation. This letter shows that curving a long monopole creates a horizontally-polarized radiation channel much less affected by this cancellation. Closed-form bounds derived from the space-wave field over a homogeneous half-space show that the vertical-current field is suppressed below an elevation set by the soil permittivity, whereas the horizontally-polarized field of an elevated arc is nearly unaffected by the soil. Bayesian optimization (BO) of an analytical model, followed by 182 full-wave HFSS simulations with analytical Fresnel far-field post-processing, was used to design the element at 15 MHz. Over moist soil, a 15-m element with an 86 degrees arc is near-resonant (-25-dB match), 93% efficient, and 25% lower than a straight element of equal length. It raises the azimuth-averaged 3 degrees-30 degrees gain by 2.5 dB and, on every land ground considered, exceeds the 0.575λ straight monopole that is optimal over seawater and PEC by 1.0-2.6 dB.

</details>

Abstract: The broadcast nature of integrated sensing and communication (ISAC) signals exposes them to physical-layer security (PLS) threats, including eavesdropping and jamming. Conventional fixed-position antennas provide limited spatial flexibility under highly correlated legitimate, sensing, and adversarial channels, while multi-static cooperative ISAC offers distributed spatial diversity but remains vulnerable to information leakage. To address these limitations, this article proposes a novel movable antenna (MA)-enabled multi-static cooperative secure ISAC framework centered on dual-scale spatial reconfiguration. Specifically, continuous MA repositioning reshapes local propagation channels, while distributed multi-static nodes provide complementary network-level spatial diversity. Their synergy transforms local antenna geometry and distributed network geometry into a unified controllable spatial resource for jointly enhancing communication, sensing, and PLS. This article first presents the system architecture of the proposed framework and its representative application scenarios, followed by an analysis of the physical-layer mechanisms underlying the resulting gains. Subsequently, we elaborate on the key enabling technologies for practical deployment. A case study further demonstrates the communication-sensing-security trade-off and antenna-saving potential of the proposed framework. Finally, open challenges and future research directions are identified toward practical MA-enabled multi-static secure ISAC networks.

Abstract: Integrated sensing and communication (ISAC) is envisioned as a cornerstone technology for next-generation networks, yet it introduces significant security risks in both communication and sensing domains. To mitigate the coverage limitations inherent in conventional mono-static or bi-static setups, this paper proposes a networked ISAC framework empowered by movable antennas (MAs). By leveraging flexible antenna reconfiguration, MAs provide additional spatial degrees of freedom to significantly boost beamforming gain and interference suppression. Specifically, we investigate a novel dual-domain security paradigm where networked base stations collaboratively protect uplink (UL) and downlink (DL) transmissions from communication eavesdropping while simultaneously safeguarding target sensing data against sensing eavesdroppers (SEs). To this end, we formulate a joint optimization problem to maximize the sum of secrecy rates, subject to sensing requirements, SE eavesdropping probability constraint, and practical MA deployment constraints, by jointly optimizing receive combiners, transmit beamformers, UL power allocation, and MA positions. To address this non-convex problem with highly coupled variables, an efficient alternating optimization algorithm is proposed, integrating closed-form solutions for receive combiners, successive convex approximation for transmit beamforming and UL power, and a tailored sensing-aware multi-candidate particle swarm optimization for the high-dimensional MA positioning. Extensive numerical results validate the effectiveness of the proposed algorithms, demonstrating that the proposed MA-enabled networked cooperative ISAC design significantly outperforms conventional fixed-position antenna-based designs in terms of security performance, while achieving notable power- and antenna-saving gains.

</details>

Abstract: This paper investigates discrete-activation pinching antenna (DAPA) systems, where a finite set of pinching antennas (PAs) is deployed along a dielectric waveguide, from which PAs are selectively activated to serve users. Performance analyses are developed for both orthogonal multiple access (OMA) and non-orthogonal multiple access (NOMA) by explicitly accounting for the system geometry and discrete PA activation. Specifically, analytical expressions for the outage probability (OP) and ergodic rate (ER) are derived. Furthermore, the discrete-to-continuous gain ratio (DCGR) is introduced to quantify the normalized ER gain of a finite PA deployment relative to the single-PA and continuous-activation references. Monte Carlo simulations validate the derived expressions and show that DAPA systems can closely approach continuous-activation performance with only a moderate number of candidate PAs. For example, under the considered setup, DAPA with five PAs recovers approximately 92% and 90% of the continuous-activation ER gain under OMA and NOMA, respectively, at a transmit power of 0 dBm.

Abstract: Maternal and perinatal deaths are concentrated in low- and middle-income countries, where routine fetal monitoring is largely inaccessible. With the prevalence of smartphone as personal assistive gadget, we ask whether a phone's built-in microphone, with no extra hardware or emitted signal, can serve as a software-only maternal-fetal cardiac screening aid when moved from the chest to the lower abdomen? We report a longitudinal, self-recorded case study of a single subject (Z) over the last five weeks of a singleton pregnancy, later delivered at term without complications: 28 short, uncontrolled recordings (rest, morning, post-exercise). Lacking maternal/fetal ground truth, we exploit the prior that maternal and fetal heart rates occupy largely disjoint frequency bands, blindly decomposing a Shannon-energy envelope into two candidate-rate bands, and additionally probe a slower breathing-rate band as a rest/exercise discriminator. A fast, regular, fetal-plausible rhythm was recovered from most recordings and remained essentially unchanged across maternal activity states, consistent with normal fetal cardiac autonomy; a slower, noisier maternal-plausible rhythm was recovered less reliably. Beat-to-beat variability proved largely noise-dominated at our short window lengths, and the breathing-rate probe did not significantly separate rest from exercise; both are reported as negative findings. We position the work within AI for global health: a low-cost screening aid for remote and underserved communities, pending reference-validated follow-up, not a replacement for clinical monitoring.

</details>

Abstract: The beam squint effect has traditionally been viewed as detrimental to near-field wideband beamforming due to the reduction of beamforming gain towards the desired direction. In this paper, we show that it can instead be harnessed as a benefit in a hardware-constrained orthogonal frequency division multiple access (OFDMA) system, where the system, equipped with a single radio-frequency (RF) chain, aims to serve multiple spatially distributed users by allocating different subcarriers to different users. By exploiting the beam squint effect, flexible frequency-dependent beamfocusing patterns are realized via parallel true-time-delay (TTD) units, enabling nearly full beamforming gain across users. In particular, we formulate a max-min rate optimization problem that jointly designs the time delays, subcarrier-user association, analog beamforming, and power allocation. To tackle the resulting nonconvex mixed-integer problem, a two-stage optimization framework is proposed. In Stage~I, subcarrier-user association and structured analog beamforming are jointly optimized. In Stage~II, power allocation is obtained via bisection search and inverse water-filling. Simulation results demonstrate that the proposed method can achieve a significant improvement by utilizing beam squint to serve multiple spatially distributed users in OFDMA systems.

</details>

Abstract: Native real-time audio engines meet audio deadlines but are hard to connect to machine-learning workflows, while tensor frameworks integrate with machine learning but process audio as offline batches without timing guarantees; runtime layers built on them inherit the host's scheduling and deployment footprint. This paper presents Fluxion, a Rust audio DSP runtime for cascaded second-order sections, FIR filters, and delay-line effects. Fluxion implements the parts that are specific to DSP: closed-form filter design, forward kernels, analytic adjoints for the recursion, and stability checks. It relies on existing libraries for the autodiff graph, GPU code generation (tested here on CUDA), and array interchange. One graph algebra is lowered to a differentiable training engine and to an allocation-free real-time engine; between them, a trained graph is checked for stability and frozen into a versioned artifact that an edge device checks again before playback. We compare Fluxion with six framework-hosted and native baselines under one float32 protocol. Among the systems that compute the exact recursion, Fluxion has the highest GPU throughput when data stays on the device and the highest throughput on most CPU workloads, while its CPU training step is slower than those of torchaudio and GRAFX. Its test suite checks the audio thread for allocations with an instrumented allocator, and we report measured latency tails. Fluxion is open source at \url{https://github.com/matteospanio/fluxion}.

</details>

Abstract: Internet of Things (IoT) networks require reliable and spectrally efficient wireless links to support massive device connectivity and high mobility applications. To meet these requirements, this paper investigates a widely linear (WL)-empowered receiver design for affine frequency division multiplexing (AFDM) with faster-than-Nyquist (FTN) signaling under practical receiver in phase and quadrature imbalance (IQI). The considered AFDM-FTN system couples the direct and image branches with FTN induced intersymbol interference (FTN-ISI) and delay-Doppler spreading in the affine domain. To address this coupled interference structure, a unified conjugate augmented input output model is established for IQI impaired AFDM-FTN systems. Based on this model, a WL minimum mean square error receiver with parallel interference cancellation (WL-MMSE-PIC) is developed, where WL-MMSE preprocessing is followed by the proposed hard decision PIC (HD-PIC) and damped soft decision PIC (SD-PIC). The proposed receiver is further analyzed in terms of the local stability of HD-PIC, a genie aided performance reference under ideal residual interference cancellation, and the impact of the AFDM chirp parameter on the post equalization interference distribution. Simulation results show that the proposed WL-MMSE-HD-PIC and WL-MMSE-SD-PIC receivers achieve significant error rate performance gains over the conventional linear-MMSE and WL-MMSE baselines. The results further show that FTN signaling provides a nominal spectral-efficiency gain, while residual-coupling-based chirp-parameter selection reduces the post-equalization residual coupling and improves detection reliability for high-mobility IoT links.

</details>

Abstract: Integrated sensing and communication (ISAC) has been recognized as a key enabler for future wireless communication systems. However, existing ISAC designs based on MIMO-OFDM generally fail to fully exploit the spatial degrees of freedom and the communication data payload, resulting in restricted target sensing performance. To cope with this issue, in this paper we propose a novel target sensing method for full-dimensional (FD) MIMO-OFDM ISAC systems based on coupled tensor decomposition. We first characterize the system model of FD MIMO-OFDM ISAC, where the base station employs a large number of antennas in two dimensions and facilitates both sensing and communication via the data payload of OFDM signals. Then, we formulate the problem of estimating target parameters as a coupled canonical polyadic decomposition (CPD) problem, which jointly leverages the echo signals of communication data payload over multiple subcarriers in the frequency domain. The theoretical analysis indicates that the formulated coupled CPD problem possesses a more relaxed uniqueness condition compared with the ordinary CPD problem, thereby enabling more stable target sensing performance through the pure communication data payload in FD MIMO-OFDM ISAC. In addition, we develop a coupled CPD-based target sensing algorithm, where the target parameters, including azimuth and elevation angles, ranges, and velocities, are extracted from the decomposed factor matrices. Numerical results show that the proposed target sensing method achieves a higher success rate and more accurate parameter estimation than existing tensor decomposition methods.

</details>

Abstract: Electroencephalographic (EEG) functional connectivity is quantified using diverse measures of statistical dependence, spectral coupling, phase synchronization, information transfer, directed interactions, cross-frequency coupling, and network topology. An important question is what can be inferred about one connectivity measure from another. This paper develops a mathematical framework that treats connectivity measures as mappings from an explicitly defined admissible state space to observable values. For a source measure and a target measure, the set of target values compatible with an observed source value is defined as a cross-connectivity attainable set. This formulation distinguishes exact determination, conditional determination using additional information, bounded inference, approximate identification, and non-identifiability. The framework is extended to multichannel connectivity matrices, graph measures, and dynamic connectivity. It also incorporates the influence of filtering, referencing, volume conduction, source leakage, inverse modeling, epoch duration, and estimator assumptions on admissible inferences. Analytical examples examine relationships between covariance and correlation, mutual information and correlation under joint Gaussianity, coherence and phase-locking value under restrictive signal conditions, and the general non-identifiability of the phase-lag index and weighted phase-lag index without additional assumptions. By making assumptions and attainable values explicit, the framework provides a systematic basis for determining what connectivity results can and cannot establish about other measures.

</details>

Abstract: The wide footprint of low-Earth-orbit (LEO) satellite downlinks exposes the mere occurrence of a transmission to any receiver within it. This paper studies covert multiuser LEO downlinks, where a passive warden at an unknown but fixed ground location accumulates observations as the satellite moves. We jointly optimize the precoding and the occurrence probabilities of the multiuser symbol combinations to provide covertness against such wardens. Each phase-shift keying (PSK) symbol vector is decomposed into a relative pattern and a common rotation, so that shaping the pattern probabilities leaves the symbols of every legitimate user uniformly distributed. We develop probabilistically shaped symbol-level precoding and probabilistically shaped block-level precoding. We derive the exact chi-square divergence of the warden's Gaussian-mixture observation over the complete session and, by bounding the leakage over a continuous location uncertainty region, obtain certificates that guarantee covertness throughout that region. The certified covertness level is minimized under robust constructive-interference, power, and entropy constraints through an alternating optimization whose updates are equivalent to convex conic problems, and the optimized probabilities follow a Maxwell--Boltzmann-type law. Numerical results show that shaping substantially reduces the certified covertness level of both precoding structures, particularly under large location uncertainty, low elevation, and scarce power.

</details>

Abstract: The need for user-specific calibration remains an often-cited barrier to widespread adoption of myoelectric control. Cross-user models trained on large multi-user datasets can now recognize gestures from unseen users without calibration, but they have been evaluated almost exclusively offline, leaving open whether calibration is still needed in closed-loop control. This study compared six calibration-free controllers, trained on 306 users of the EMG-EPN-612 dataset and used without any calibration data from the test participant, against three calibrated controllers in a closed-loop Fitts' law target-acquisition task with sixteen participants. The calibrated controllers achieved higher offline balanced accuracy (90.6% against 73.1% for the best controller of each paradigm), but completion rates did not differ significantly between paradigms, and the highest completion rate was achieved by a calibration-free controller. Calibrated controllers moved faster on successful acquisitions, partly because of their individually fitted speed mappings, though not significantly after correction for multiple comparisons. However, every calibration-free controller produced more direct and stable trajectories than every calibrated controller, even after adjusting for cursor speed. Offline accuracy did not predict completion rate across controllers or within participants, and across paradigms higher offline accuracy was associated with poorer trajectory quality in 15 of 16 participants. Without any calibration data from the end user, a model trained across a large population matched calibrated control in completion rate and exceeded it in trajectory quality. The offline accuracy gained through calibration should therefore not be taken as evidence of a closed-loop benefit.

</details>

Abstract: Multiobject tracking (MOT) is a fundamental capability in autonomous navigation, radar and sonar systems, as well as 6G integrated sensing-and-communication (ISAC) networks. In this paper, we propose a neural-enhanced track-before-detect (TkBD) framework that operates directly on complex-valued raw signals and learns a latent representation of unmodeled propagation effects during runtime. We introduce an environment-dependent latent intensity variable that characterizes the signal power of each object in the shared propagation environment. The probability density function of this intensity variable is represented by a normalizing flow and trained online in an unsupervised manner within the sequential Bayesian inference procedure. The learning objective is derived from a variational Bayes formulation by maximizing the evidence lower bound (ELBO), thereby accounting for the uncertainties inherent in the Bayesian TkBD problem. To enable scalable inference, we make use of a recently introduced Bayesian TkBD framework that combines belief propagation, which avoids high-dimensional operations, with sequential Monte Carlo methods for efficient low-dimensional updates.

Abstract: This paper considers a cell-free (CF) network under active eavesdropping. We show that nonlinear power amplifier (PA) distortion, together with the per-access point (AP) large-scale-gain fingerprint of the attack, can be exploited across cooperating APs to enhance physical layer security (PLS). Classical zero-forcing (ZF)-artificial noise (AN) precoders rely on a linear transmitter and an unbiased estimate, and thus cannot exploit either. We propose a distortion aware graph neural network (GNN) precoder that designs the signal beams at cooperating APs and exploits both the PA distortion and the per-AP fingerprint to enhance the secrecy rate, combined with a null-space AN block to form the GNN-AN scheme. The GNN is trained on channel estimates biased by an uplink pilot-spoofing attack to maximise the average per-user secrecy rate. Simulations across different numbers of APs show uniformly higher outage secrecy rates than ZF-AN under attack, reaching gains of 15% at the 1% outage level and 8.9% at the 5% outage level at L = 32 APs, input back-off (IBO) = -1 dB, and attacker-to-targeted user pilot power ratio of 0.5, under the same signal/AN power split.

Abstract: Affine frequency division multiplexing (AFDM) is a promising waveform for future high-mobility communications due to its Doppler robustness and diversity gain. However, existing continuous-time AFDM relies on chirp-dependent wideband receive filtering, which increases noise power and causes channel-energy leakage. In this paper, we propose an AFDM reception scheme based on pre-filter de-chirp for doubly selective channels. The coupling between de-chirp and the channel is first characterized, yielding an equivalent path representation with delay-coupled effective Doppler shifts. The discrete affine Fourier transform (DAFT)-domain equivalent channel is then derived under general transmit pulse shaping and linear time-invariant receive filtering, showing that the effective filtering kernel depends only on the relative sample offset and avoids the additional spreading caused by conventional chirp-dependent filtering. The receive-noise power is further analyzed, and an explicit expression for the signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) gap is obtained by the required receive-filter bandwidths. Simulations verify the theoretical SNR analysis and show that the proposed scheme significantly outperforms the conventional scheme while approaching ideal discrete-time AFDM.

Abstract: Accurate radio-based indoor localization remains challenging in multipath-rich and dynamic indoor environments. Supervised fingerprinting methods achieve high positioning accuracy by learning mappings from radio channel measurements to physical locations, but require costly position labels and frequent recalibration. Channel Charting (CC) addresses this limitation by learning low-dimensional representations of Channel State Information (CSI) without position labels, yielding channel charts in which nearby points correspond to nearby physical locations. However, prominent CC methods rely on setup-specific dissimilarity metrics, such as ADP-, CIRA-, or PDR-based distances, and on precomputed $O(n^2)$ distance matrices, limiting scalability and transferability across radio systems. We propose ChronoChart, a timestamp-supervised CC framework that requires only CSI and timestamps without prior knowledge for training. Our method jointly optimizes complementary objectives: a triplet loss using temporally derived positive and negative samples to establish coarse global structure, a temporal-proximity loss that promotes smooth trajectories in the chart, and a joint-embedding loss that enforces consistency between representations of different augmentations. Evaluated on real-world 5G, MIMO, and passive UWB data, our method matches or exceeds prior CC baselines that depend on tailored distance metrics, and remains scalable and applicable across heterogeneous radio systems.

</details>

Abstract: Reliable radio-based indoor localization is essential for industrial automation, yet existing methods are often evaluated in environments that do not fully reflect the severe multipath and non-line-of-sight (NLOS) conditions encountered in real deployments. This limits our understanding of how AI, i.e., modern geometric, unsupervised, and semi-supervised localization methods generalize to industrial 5G settings. We present RAIL-5G, a public real-world 5G NR channel impulse response (CIR) benchmark recorded in an operational railway maintenance facility. RAIL-5G covers an 811m$^2$ indoor area with eight distributed radio units, robot-mounted user equipment (UE) trajectories, high-accuracy iGPS reference positions, and severe metal-induced multipath caused by train compartments and maintenance infrastructure. We benchmark geometric time-difference of arrival (TDoA), supervised fingerprinting, unsupervised channel charting, and self-supervised wireless pretraining methods. Our results highlight the difficulty of RAIL-5G: on the full dataset, TDoA reaches 7.02m mean average error (MAE), the best unsupervised method reaches 2.46m, and supervised fingerprinting achieves 0.52m. Self-supervised pretraining reduces the labeling effort by up to one order of magnitude, but the remaining gap to supervised learning shows that robust AI-based localization under severe multipath remains an open challenge. The dataset and code are available at https://iis.fraunhofer.de/rail-5g.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the sampling of bandlimited signals on multigraphs, where several graphs with different shift operators are defined on the same set of nodes. Since the shift operators do not commute in general, they admit only a joint block diagonalization, and the frequencies of the multigraph signal model are matrices rather than scalars. Relying on a formal algebraic convolutional signal model, we introduce a notion of frequency and bandwidth for multigraph signals that does not depend on any individual layer, and we extend the notions of removable and uniqueness sets to multigraphs. We prove that the optimality of a sampling set is determined exclusively by the common Fourier basis and its invariant subspaces, and not by any individual layer, although the individual layers provide computable sufficient conditions that certify a sampling set as a uniqueness set. Since identifying which layers provide reliable certificates requires spectral information, we propose an aggregate operator that pools the information of all the layers, is computable directly from the shift operators, and certifies uniqueness without any assumption on the individual layers. This operator leads to a greedy sampling algorithm that does not require computing the joint block diagonalization. Numerical experiments on multigraphs whose layers order the frequencies inconsistently show that the proposed algorithm selects better-conditioned sampling sets and attains lower reconstruction errors than single-layer methods.

</details>

Abstract: Hybrid non-orthogonal multiple access (H-NOMA), which leverages the complementary advantages of orthogonal multiple access (OMA) and NOMA, has emerged as a promising technique for next-generation wireless networks. This paper investigates power allocation optimization for the OMA and NOMA time slots allocated to the opportunistic user in H-NOMA systems, aiming to overcome the limitations of conventional equal power allocation strategies. Specifically, we consider an H-NOMA scheme that integrates hybrid successive interference cancellation (HSIC) with power adaptation (PA), referred to as the HSIC-PA aided H-NOMA scheme. To better adapt to practical communication scenarios, distinct power coefficients are assigned to the OMA and NOMA time slots of the opportunistic user to maximize the achievable data rate. Closed-form expressions for the optimal power allocation coefficients are derived, and simulation results validate the accuracy and effectiveness of the theoretical analysis.

</details>

Abstract: Coherent distributed radar architectures enhance the detectability of low-reflectivity targets. However, conventional coherent processing generally entails prohibitively large volumes of inter-station data exchange, thereby motivating the adoption of one-bit quantization strategies. In this work, we investigate a clutter-dominated regime in which the interference is driven by a dense ensemble of weak scatterers. We demonstrate that, under these conditions, one-bit quantization can exhibit a vanishing performance loss with respect to ideal high-resolution sampling in terms of detection capability, in contrast to the classical 2 dB performance penalty associated with one-bit detection in white Gaussian noise environments. For unknown target velocity, we reformulate joint velocity-search processing under the effective one-bit model. Maximizing over the velocity plane makes false-alarm calibration an extreme-value problem for a correlated two-dimensional random field, whose maximum is not characterized by its pointwise null distribution alone. We therefore use an expected Euler characteristic approximation to calibrate the search threshold. We obtain analytic expressions for a separable two-station geometry and use constrained coefficient fitting for larger networks. Monte Carlo simulations demonstrate negligible quantization loss in the clutter-dominated setting and conservative false-alarm calibration under the tested Gaussian slow-time model.

</details>

Abstract: Time-interleaved analog-to-digital converters (TI-ADCs) enable multi-gigasample-per-second throughput in high-speed applications, but sub-picosecond timing skews among interleaved channels introduce large spectral artifacts that degrade dynamic performance. To continuously track parametric drifts without interrupting normal operation, background calibration is required. However, extracting timing skews under unknown inputs is challenging, and many existing correlation-based methods rely on resource-heavy derivative filters that restrict input bandwidth. This article proposes a novel background timing-skew estimation technique for two-channel TI-ADCs that overcomes these limitations. Closed-form timing-skew expressions are derived using: (a) adjacent-channel cross-correlation combined with single-channel auto-correlation for sinusoidal inputs, and (b) adjacent-channel cross-correlation for Wide-Sense Stationary (WSS) inputs, specifically bandpass-filtered wideband noise and Orthogonal Frequency Division Multiplexed (OFDM) signals. The derived analytical models are validated through comprehensive simulations, offering an efficient framework for real-time background calibration in high-speed data converters.

Abstract: Most existing studies on pinching-antenna systems optimize the antenna positions or activation states under a prescribed waveguide deployment, where mutually parallel waveguides are commonly adopted. However, it remains unclear whether, and under what conditions, such a deployment is optimal from a communication-performance perspective. To fill this gap, this paper investigates long-term waveguide deployment optimization for pinching-antenna systems over a rectangular service region. We first consider the fundamental two-waveguide case and derive a closed-form sufficient condition, jointly determined by the waveguide height and the service-region aspect ratio, under which a deployment parallel to the longer side of the service region is globally optimal. We then consider the general multi-waveguide case, where the orientation and offset of each waveguide are jointly optimized without prescribing any deployment pattern, and develop an adaptive exchange algorithm for the resulting nonconvex optimization problem. We further show that, within the parallel class, the waveguides should be aligned with the longer side of the service region, while in the large-height regime, the globally optimal unrestricted deployment asymptotically concentrates all waveguides on the centerline along the longer side. Numerical results validate the proposed algorithm and reveal how the preferred deployment geometry depends on the waveguide height and service-region shape. In particular, nonparallel deployments can provide noticeable gains in intermediate-height regimes, whereas parallel or center-concentrated deployments become favorable in other regimes.

</details>

Abstract: Single-photon avalanche diode (SPAD) arrays offer photon-level sensitivity for sensing in optical integrated sensing and communication (O-ISAC). However, after each detection, a SPAD is blind for a dead time, which saturates the array at high received power. Existing O-ISAC analyses assume linear receivers and do not capture this effect. In this paper, we study ranging with a SPAD array in a monostatic O-ISAC system based on direct-current-biased optical orthogonal frequency-division multiplexing (DCO-OFDM). Sampling the array once per dead time, we model the number of pixels that detect a photon in each sampling interval as a binomial random variable whose success probability accounts for the intensity variation of the echo within a dead time. Based on this model, we derive the Cramér-Rao bound (CRB) for range estimation. We show that the SPAD count model of optical wireless communication, which assumes a constant photon rate over a dead time, causes a range bias that depends on the received power. Moreover, unlike for a linear photodiode, saturation degrades the ranging accuracy beyond a sensing-optimal transmit power. The rate-constrained operating point is then the larger of this power and the power needed for the target data rate. Numerical results validate the analysis against a continuous-time photon-counting simulation and show that the proposed model is needed for ranging.

</details>

Abstract: GNSS signal acquisition is the process of detecting and estimating signal parameters by exploiting correlation operations that provide processing gain and enable the detection of signals below the noise floor. The design of an acquisition engine requires balancing achievable sensitivity, computational complexity, memory requirements, power consumption, and implementation complexity. In particular, increasing coherent and non-coherent integration times improves sensitivity but can significantly increase receiver processing requirements, limiting its applicability in low-power and resource-constrained receivers.
  This paper presents a comparative analysis of L1-band signal acquisition complexity by evaluating the required integration time and corresponding implementation requirements for different signal designs under equivalent acquisition performance targets. The analysis considers the impact of signal characteristics, including spreading codes, secondary codes, modulation, and signal bandwidth, on coherent and non-coherent acquisition performance. For a given attenuation level and target probability of false alarm and detection, the required integration times are derived and translated into acquisition complexity metrics, including arithmetic operations, processing time, and memory requirements.
  The analysis is applied to Pulsar X1, a LEO-enabled PNT signal, and representative GNSS L1 signals to determine under which attenuation, assistance, and integration conditions the higher received power of LEO-PNT translates into reduced receiver acquisition workload. By relating the required integration to arithmetic operations, processing time, and memory requirements, the study provides a receiver-level comparison relevant to both conventional and snapshot-based receivers, where reducing acquisition processing can directly contribute to lower energy consumption.

Abstract: We develop a vision--radio-frequency (RF) cooperative integrated sensing and communication (ISAC) framework that jointly uses visual priors and RF observations for transmit-side beam control and receiver-side clutter suppression. Within a narrowband snapshot model, visual target priors and historical RF detections guide control in dynamic vehicle-to-infrastructure networks with roadside clutter. In this framework, posterior angular uncertainty determines beam control and the selection between communication-for-sensing and sensing-for-sensing modes. To reduce sensing overhead while accommodating unreliable visual information, we develop a reliability-adaptive beam scanning scheme that allocates a bounded scan budget according to visual target probabilities and RF history, while reserving an adaptive fraction for exploration. At the receiver, visual geometry identifies clutter support, and RF observations estimate the corresponding complex reflection coefficients. Motion-aware residual filtering is then applied before angle--Doppler detection to suppress clutter while preserving slow-moving targets. Furthermore, communication and sensing power allocation is formulated as a per-direction linear program, subject to signal-to-interference-plus-noise ratio constraints evaluated at angles sampled from the posterior distribution and a total-power budget. Simulation results show that the proposed framework improves tracking over static and vision-only beam control and reduces sensing overhead relative to full scanning. The proposed scanning scheme also provides greater robustness to visual blockage than fixed-weight scanning, while the receiver better preserves slow-moving targets than temporal cancellation when visual target information is available.

Abstract: Artificial intelligence (AI) is increasingly applied to 5G/6G network optimization, yet differences in empirical evidence, evaluation methods, and computational requirements complicate comparisons of reported performance. This scop ing review synthesizes 72 peer-reviewed journal studies pub lished between 2022 and 2026, selected through a PRISMA ScR-informed process from 893 records. The review examines empirical tools, AI/ML techniques, optimization domains, and operational readiness. Evidence classification identifies 37 studies (51.4%) using real-world or physical-testbed evidence only, 25 (34.7%) combining measured and simulated evidence, and 10 (13.9%) relying exclusively on synthetic or simulated data. Offline analysis was the most common evaluation setting, accounting for 45 studies (62.5%). Three principal findings emerge. First, empirical measurements improve contextual relevance, while simulation and digital twins support scalable experimentation but introduce validation challenges. Second, algorithmic effectiveness depends on the optimization objective, data characteristics, and computational constraints rather than model complexity alone. Third, predictive accuracy is more consistently evaluated than the network-level effects of optimization decisions. The review there fore introduces the six-module Evidence Fidelity and Operational Reporting (EFOR) checklist to improve evaluation consistency, reporting transparency, and operational relevance.

</details>

Abstract: Phase modulated continuous wave (PMCW) radar is an attractive choice for automotive sensing due to its high dynamic range, interference robustness, and support for integrated communication and sensing. In multiple-input multiple-output (MIMO) PMCW radar, code division multiplexing (CDM) separates transmit signals, but non-ideal auto- and cross-correlation properties increase range sidelobes and degrade dynamic range. This paper proposes a Doppler-compensated space-time coding (STC)-based MIMO PMCW radar framework that separates transmit signals before range correlation, eliminating cross-correlation terms under perfect compensation and allowing the same binary sequence to be used across transmit antennas. The Doppler influence on STC decoding is analytically characterized, showing that chip-wise and inter-STC-slot phase distortions increase range sidelobes and generate ghost components in the direction-of-arrival (DOA) spectrum. To mitigate these effects, Doppler is estimated first, followed by joint fast-time and STC-slot compensation before decoding. Residual STC leakage under imperfect Doppler estimation is quantified, and the processing is extended to multiple moving targets through Doppler-bin-dependent compensation. Theoretical analysis and simulation results demonstrate reduced range sidelobes and suppression of DOA ghosts, while maintaining flexibility in code sequence design.

</details>

Abstract: LLM agents are widely used for software development and debugging, but their adoption in embedded systems, especially for wireless tasks, remains limited. Across four increasingly complex tasks, from generating a carrier to backscatter decoding by a commercial receiver, and using six models from two vendors, hardware-in-the-loop increases task completion rates from 54% to 79% and from 17% to 83% on the most challenging task. Abstract: Reliable indoor positioning remains a central unsolved problem in spatial computing. Existing systems depend on fixed, provider-owned infrastructure (WiFi access points, Bluetooth beacons, UWB anchors) whose deployment, calibration, and ownership models limit their scalability. We present TrackerNet, a vision for transforming personal trackers from private object-finders into a decentralized substrate for ubiquitous positioning. We identify three research frontiers (non-stationary SLAM over mobile, intermittently visible anchors; semantic reasoning that connects ranging signals to interpretations of anchor behavior; and consent-based, privacy-preserving governance for cross-vendor cooperation) and a staged pathway by which the vendors and venue operators who already run tracker networks can realize them with academia. We do not claim a finished system; we define a research agenda.

Abstract: Intent-Based Networking (IBN) is a key enabler of autonomous network management in next-generation networks, as it allows operators to drive the network through high-level intents instead of low-level configuration commands. In this paper, we propose a semantic routing framework for telecom intent classification and translation across the radio access networks (RANs), transport, 5G core, and cloud/edge domains. This framework operates locally to preserve intent privacy and avoid the latency and cost of cloud-based Large Language Model (LLM) solutions. The proposed framework is composed of intent classification and translation. To accurately classify the intent, we propose fine-tuning sentence-transformer models using contrastive learning over a 3GPP TS 28.312-aligned taxonomy of five top-level routes and 22 branches, supported by a telecom-specific codebook of 1,100 intents. In addition, we develop a branchaware, template-driven rule-based module for intent translation that enforces deterministic, hallucination-free parameter extraction and explicit missing-field detection, ensuring a structured and verifiable output pipeline. The numerical results show that the best fine-tuned encoder achieves up to 99.02%/94.61% route/branch classification accuracy, outperforming both base encoders and a commercial embedding baseline. The proposed translator achieves up to 96.24% translation accuracy, compared to 85.37% for a cloud-based LLM benchmark. Moreover, since the proposed pipeline runs entirely on local hardware, an average translation latency of 4.81 ms is recorded against 4,163.58 ms for the cloud-based benchmark, i.e., a speedup of approximately 866x.

Abstract: Mobile networks are evolving from human-centered, data-oriented communication infrastructures toward machine- and/or agent-dominant, task-oriented platforms that are to serve the rapidly increasing networked machines and embodied agents. In order to fulfill the newly arose complex task objectives, the framework of future mobile network is to be innovated to reflect and facilitate the intrinsic interplay and integration of the underlying processes of perception, communication, computation, decision, action, and feedback, etc. This survey establishes the mobile embodied agent network (MEAN) as a task-oriented networking paradigm in which networking and distributed intelligence are jointly organized around closed-loop embodied task execution. We first establish a unified MEAN framework that explains how network resources and intelligent functions jointly support embodied task execution, and extend its performance indicators from conventional communication metrics to embodied task outcomes and cross-layer system effects. With this framework, we analyze three complementary enabling mechanisms that form a cognition-interaction-adaption loop. Large artificial intelligence (AI) models provide high-level cognition, token communication connects this cognition with distributed agents through shared semantic representations, and reinforcement learning closes the loop by adapting system operation to network, task, and physical feedback. We further investigate the bidirectional interplay between MEAN and emerging technologies, showing how they mutually extend system capabilities and reshape task-oriented network design. Finally, we discuss open challenges and future research directions toward the practical realization of MEAN over real joint communication and computation platform.

Abstract: Intelligent agent interactions in real-world web environments are commonly constrained by request budgets, time delays, anti-crawling restrictions, and operational failure risks. Strategies solely optimizing task success rates often exhibit unusable phenomena such as "high success but high cost" or "low risk but conservative failure." This paper proposes a constrained agentic reinforcement learning model for web agents, unifying page access, search requests, and external API calls into a unified long-term decision-making framework with associated costs. It simultaneously incorporates cost budget constraints and tail risk control into the optimization objective: constructing a multidimensional cost vector comprising cumulative request count, total latency, and failure penalties to achieve budget compliance via Lagrange dual updates, while employing a CVaR risk term to suppress excessive exploration of high-failure-probability paths, thereby achieving an adaptive balance among "completion rate, cost, and risk."
  Experiments were conducted across 30-70 site/page templates and 800-1,500 end-to-end web tasks, including information extraction, price comparison, form submission, and cross-page navigation. Interaction sequences spanned 20-120 steps with tool scales of 30-200. Performance was benchmarked against unconstrained RL, budget-constrained RL, and rule-based/scripted web agents, quantifying task completion rates, cost-per-success, failure rates, and policy stability.
  Results demonstrate that at equivalent completion rates, our method reduces C-PS by 22%-31% and lowers failure rates by 18%-26% under high failure penalties. Under fixed budgets, task completion rates increase by 10%-16%, highlighting the necessity and effectiveness of constraint modeling for practical web agent deployment.

</details>

Abstract: Adaptive speculative decoding changes the active batch size B and draft length d from one decode step to the next, whereas a CUDA Graph replays a fixed launch configuration. The resulting verification width M = B d forces serving systems to pre-capture many shapes, capture new ones on the serving path, or pad every step to the largest draft. We present PolyGraph, a plan-buffer mechanism for custom Triton kernels that avoids this choice. PolyGraph captures one maximum-grid graph and writes the active shape to GPU memory before each replay. Kernels skip tiles beyond that shape, while graph topology, allocations, layouts, pointers, and launch grids remain fixed; the update transfers only 28 bytes. On one NVIDIA H800, a trace-driven prototype with Qwen2.5-3B dimensions replaces 64 shape-specific graphs with one, matches the warmed baseline in observed step latency (17.7 vs. 17.8 ms P50), and reduces measured pre-serving graph-build time from 3,915 to 93 ms. Fixed-draft controls show that the benefit comes from avoiding padded payload: the P99 difference from MaxPad is negligible at zero padding waste but reaches an 18.7% reduction at 100% waste. Our evidence establishes a bounded work-shape mechanism for custom kernels, not a production serving system, and is limited to synthetic traces on one GPU.

</details>

Abstract: We introduce a predictive world model for electronic health records (EHR) that represents patient histories as action conditioned latent trajectories. Rather than modeling clinical events solely through autoregressive token prediction, our framework explicitly separates patient states from observed clinical actions and learns their evolution through a state and action conditioned transition operator. At its core is a state dependent linearization predictor that propagates local changes in latent patient state, enabling time resolved prediction, multi step rollout, and action conditioned simulation. Empirically, we show that generated latent trajectories preserve downstream predictive signal, temporal structure, and subgroup level statistical relationships present in encoder derived representations. We further decode free running rollouts into the original clinical feature space and evaluate future clinical measurements against observed values using task matched forecasting baselines, demonstrating that the learned dynamics retain predictive information beyond latent space agreement. Across binary classification and time to event tasks, EHR WM achieves competitive, though task dependent, performance relative to substantially larger EHR foundation models while using a 30x smaller parameter footprint. Finally, perturbing observed action sequences produces corresponding changes in generated trajectories, demonstrating the action sensitivity of the learned dynamics. Together, these results support world modeling as a complementary framework for learning explicit, time indexed representations of patient evolution from longitudinal EHR.

Abstract: Adaptive VLM ensembles save computation by stopping early on easy images and escalating harder ones. Although a single conformal threshold can make the final predictor marginally valid, it can still fail severely along individual computational routes. We characterize this failure precisely: if a route has prevalence pi, marginal 1-alpha coverage allows its conditional coverage to fall to max{0, 1-alpha/pi}. To address this mismatch, we learn the router on development data, freeze all routing choices, and calibrate the returned score separately for early exits and escalated inputs. We call this pipeline SAGE-CP. Its calibration step applies Mondrian split conformal prediction to the operational groups created by the router. Under exchangeability, the resulting prediction sets achieve finite-sample coverage within each route. Across 10 benchmarks, 4 frozen VLMs, and 5 aligned splits, both global calibration and SAGE-CP achieve 0.900 marginal coverage at a 0.90 target, but their route behavior differs substantially. SAGE-CP raises mean worst-route coverage from 0.623 to 0.887 and reduces the mean route-coverage gap from 0.324 to 0.025, using the same routed scores and 1.177 source calls per image. The stronger guarantee increases the mean prediction-set size from 1.893 to 2.212; nevertheless, the sets remain smaller than those of the full ensemble (2.486) while requiring 70.6 percent fewer source calls. The route-level reliability persists across 6 fixed query fractions, while finer exit-depth groups demonstrate the need for additional calibration data on rare routes. Across 5 datasets, SAGE-CP also reduces measured batch-one latency by 58.8 to 63.4 percent and gross GPU energy by 55.7 to 57.5 percent relative to full inference. Thus, routing provides computational savings, while route-stratified calibration attaches coverage guarantees to the operational paths created by the router.

</details>

Abstract: Tabular data are prevalent across diverse domains such as healthcare and finance. Zero-shot tabular learning aims to make predictions without labeled data. However, existing LLM-based approaches rely on row-wise prompting, resulting in high inference cost and limited use of inter-row dependencies. In this work, we propose TABHGR+, an efficient relation-aware zero-shot framework for tabular data that leverages LLM-generated rules to label and group rows. Specifically, based on column and task descriptions, we use LLMs to generate rules that assign weak labels to all rows. To refine these noisy weak labels, we construct a hypergraph by grouping semantically related rows using LLM-generated rules and perform hypergraph-based label propagation. Lastly, we further enhance the LLM-generated hypergraph for propagation, which is potentially sub-optimal, by assigning label-adaptive weights to hyperedges. By requiring only a constant number of LLM calls independent of dataset size, TABHGR+ substantially reduces inference cost while achieving higher classification accuracy than zero-shot and strong few-shot baselines.

Abstract: When using a large language model (LLM) to simulate a user population, how should we combine individual behavioral histories to predict the fraction of users who take a particular action? We study this question in e-commerce, predicting observed product-level conversion rates from browsing sessions without task-specific model training. We vary two design choices, each involving a bias-variance tradeoff. Summarizing a product's sessions before inference reduces cost but can discard predictive detail, adding bias, whereas predicting outcomes for individual sessions and averaging afterward preserves that detail but requires many calls to reduce variance. Evaluating products together in one prompt can help the model infer what is typical in the setting, reducing bias, but also makes each prediction depend on which other products appear alongside it, adding noise. We evaluate both choices across five LLMs and two datasets. Our main finding is that predictions from individual sessions outperform predictions from summaries when products are evaluated separately, but summaries lead when products are evaluated together. Comparative summaries achieve the highest Spearman correlation with observed rates in 12 of 15 settings, with no alternative significantly better. Comparison thus helps summaries far more than session predictions, overcoming the advantage of behavioral detail. We interpret this difference through a Bayesian model of the second tradeoff, which suggests that comparison helps more when the inputs in a prompt reveal more about differences across products, consistent with our results for four types of comparative context. Comparative summaries also incur a small fraction of the inference cost of the session methods. With the best model for each outcome, they attain 66-89% of the correlation of the best supervised benchmark trained on thousands of labeled products.

</details>

Abstract: Speculative decoding accelerates large language model inference through parallel verification of tokens proposed by a drafter. Diffusion drafters further parallelize proposal generation, but greater proposal capacity or higher mean accepted tokens (MAT) need not yield higher throughput. Because verification accepts only a consecutive prefix, early rejections can render later correct predictions ineffective, limiting MAT gains, while even higher MAT may not improve throughput if per-round latency increases. We introduce DScout, which uses two complementary factorizations to guide diffusion drafter training and inference for higher throughput. For training, DScout factorizes position-wise loss into inclusion, weighting, and prediction loss. This guides the design and combination of mixed-horizon training, intra-block unmasking, and acceptance-weighted loss to align supervision with prefix acceptance and improve MAT. Abstract: In-context learning (ICL) enables language models to make accurate predictions from demonstrations, but prediction success alone does not reveal the extent to which the underlying concept has been recovered. Existing evaluations typically assess recovery at the level of the whole concept, making it difficult to identify which components are recovered and to separate model limitations from ambiguity in the demonstrations. We introduce a component-level framework for concept recovery that decomposes concepts into their defining components and uses controlled structural interventions to evaluate both component-level and whole-concept recovery under identifiable demonstrations. Across four instruction-tuned LLMs and three composition forms, we find that models often achieve high prediction accuracy despite poor whole-concept recovery, with systematic over-specification of relevant attributes and level constraints. Individual structural components are generally insufficient for whole-concept recovery, whereas jointly specifying complementary components, particularly relevant attributes and their level constraints, substantially improves recovery. However, this improvement is fragile: a single plausible but unnecessary level constraint can substantially disrupt recovery. Finally, joint supervision of recovery and implicit prediction substantially improves recovery while preserving prediction performance, with the learned capability transferring across composition forms. Overall, our component-level analysis provides a more diagnostic view of what models recover from demonstrations than the existing holistic view.

</details>

Abstract: Acoustic models for cough detection achieve high accuracy on curated benchmarks but fail silently in deployment, where speech, environmental noise, and audio unlike the training data induce confident misclassifications. In health monitoring, a confidently wrong prediction is worse than no answer at all. We present SECURE-AI (Self-aware Estimation of Cough Uncertainty for Reliable Evaluation of AI), a multi-signal uncertainty quantification framework that wraps a frozen MobileNetV3 cough classifier and estimates when its predictions should not be trusted, combining Monte Carlo Dropout, Temperature Scaling, and Mahalanobis distance in the embedding space. On a held-out split of 12,161 Coswara recordings, the probability-space methods are individually insufficient: Monte Carlo Dropout assigns lower uncertainty to incorrect than to correct predictions, and temperature scaling improves calibration but not discrimination. The strongest signal is embedding-space distance: Mahalanobis distance separates true positives from false positives (means 44.7 versus 63.4; Cohen's $d = 0.94$; AUROC 0.86) and, among predicted non-coughs, false negatives from true negatives (AUROC 0.82), so the same signal can flag both false alarms and missed coughs. At a descriptive operating point selected from labeled test data, retaining 90 percent of true-positive detections removes about two-thirds of false positives and raises precision from 59.9 to roughly 80 percent. A rejection threshold tuned on one split does not transfer to another (validation F1 of 82 percent falls to 34 percent on test), so we report threshold-independent AUROC as the main measure. An error taxonomy shows that failures are systematic, with most false positives related to speech. The pipeline runs on Raspberry Pi hardware costing 30 to 90 US dollars. Probabilistic calibration alone is thus inadequate for reliable cough detection.

</details>

Abstract: The size of the KV cache grows linearly with context length, making it a major memory bottleneck in LLM serving. Motivated by recent progress in identifying the functional roles of attention heads, a growing body of work compresses the KV cache non-uniformly across heads. Existing methods either fix the important heads via offline profiling or select them using attention signals from the prefill stage. In both cases, however, accuracy fluctuates substantially across workloads. While investigating the cause of this instability, we find that the active heads shift between the prefill and decode stages even within a single input. Along the decode path, by contrast, the activehead composition remains stable across content tokens once format tokens are filtered out. Based on this observation, we propose PeekKV. PeekKV observes a single content token immediately after prefill - a one-token peek - to jointly obtain head-group scores and token-importance scores. Guided by these scores, PeekKV allocates KV cache budgets to head groups in proportion to their group scores and retains only the top-scoring tokens within each budget. PeekKV requires no offline profiling or training and thus applies directly to unseen workloads and new models. Across four public benchmarks, PeekKV achieves the highest accuracy in more than half of the per-model comparisons against state-of-the-art methods, and stays closest to the full-cache accuracy on RULER and LongBench. On Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct, PeekKV retains 95.7% of the full-cache accuracy at a 10% memory budget on RULER with 16K contexts and 95.9% at a 1.5% budget on LongBench.

</details>

Abstract: Collision detection for complex non-convex objects is a bottleneck in physics simulation, virtual reality, and robotics. Inner sphere trees (ISTs) answer proximity and penetration queries efficiently, but their greedy leaf-sphere construction is sequential and computationally expensive, which limits both large-scale preprocessing and runtime use. In this work, we present ML-IST, a machine-learning-based method that reformulates leaf-sphere generation as a single-shot set-prediction problem: a network predicts a fixed set of inscribed sphere candidates from multichannel voxel geometry, differentiable coverage feedback corrects the coarse prediction and fills residual gaps at finer scales, and a lightweight geometric projection enforces validity. Averaged over the test set, a single-GPU implementation of ML-IST reduces mean leaf-generation time by a factor of $2.0$--$2.6$ over single-core CPU construction across target capacities of 64--512 spheres, and qualitative results on complex models show multiscale sphere distributions that follow principal volumes and slender features. We further integrate ML-IST into the Jolt Physics and Bullet engines; in an alpha-puzzle benchmark it reaches 55.89~FPS, the highest among the compared representations (conventional IST, GImpact, and convex decomposition). These results support learned leaf generation as a practical component for fast, runtime construction of detailed collision representations.

Abstract: Human Activity Recognition (HAR) using wearable or smartphone inertial sensors is commonly evaluated with both classical machine learning and deep learning methods. However, reported performance can be strongly affected by sensor modality, dataset characteristics, and data-splitting strategy. This study presents a controlled comparative evaluation of four classical machine learning models and four deep learning models on two public HAR datasets, UCI-HAR and KU-HAR. Three sensor modalities were considered: accelerometer (ACC), gyroscope (GYRO), and their combination (ACC+GYRO). Classical machine learning models used handcrafted statistical features, while deep learning models operated directly on raw sensor sequences. The primary evaluation employed five-fold StratifiedGroupKFold cross-validation, with subject- or participant-level grouping to prevent group overlap between training and test folds. Macro-F1 was used as the primary performance metric. The results show that deep learning did not consistently outperform classical machine learning. On UCI-HAR, the evaluated deep learning models achieved slightly higher macro-F1 values, whereas on KU-HAR, LightGBM achieved 0.857 compared with 0.828 for the highest-performing evaluated deep learning model. Regarding sensor modality, the combined ACC+GYRO configuration generally outperformed ACC or GYRO alone on both datasets. The evaluation protocol also had a substantial effect: for CNNBiLSTMAttn with ACC+GYRO, random splitting increased macro-F1 by 0.033 on UCI-HAR and 0.093 on KU-HAR compared with grouped evaluation. These findings highlight the importance of model family, sensor modality, dataset characteristics, and group-aware evaluation when assessing sensor-based HAR systems.

Abstract: LLM agents accumulate valuable task-specific information through their actions, observations, and environmental feedback during multi-turn task execution. When effectively used, this information can help agents refine subsequent decisions and improve task performance. However, the learning value of an interaction depends on its context and subsequent feedback, making it challenging to extract reliable, actionable lessons and revise them as the task unfolds. In this paper, we introduce ReTTT, which enables experience-driven self-adaptation through test-time training on lessons constructed and continually revised within an episode. ReTTT lets the agent reflect on interaction feedback, maintain the resulting lessons in an explicit memory, and learn from them by updating an episode-local LoRA adapter while keeping the backbone frozen. The adapted model then performs both subsequent actions and reflection, coupling policy adaptation with the construction of future learning experience. We evaluate ReTTT on ALFWorld, ScienceWorld, and SWE-bench Lite using two model families. ReTTT outperforms trajectory-based test-time training across all six model--benchmark settings, improving task success by up to 12.14 percentage points. A frozen-reflection comparison further shows a 7.14-point benefit from adapting the reflection policy, while trajectory analysis illustrates how adapted reflection derives more task-specific guidance from failed attempts. These findings suggest that learning to reflect on its own behavior can boost agents' performance on long-horizon tasks.

</details>

Abstract: Pretraining scaling laws treat training data as a one-dimensional quantity: a token count. Agentic reinforcement learning (RL) inherits this scalar abstraction, but modern synthesis pipelines now generate \textit{tasks}, \textit{environments}, and \textit{trajectories} independently and at very different costs, turning data into a structured design space. This raises a question pretraining never had to answer: given a synthesis budget, \textit{which axis} should be scaled? We address this question end-to-end with three tightly coupled contributions. First, we decompose agentic data into three cardinality axes (task, environment, trajectory), plus a reward-density control variable, and fit per-axis scaling relations on different large language models (LLMs) across math+code, SWE, web, and UI/tool application domains. Across all model sizes, model families, domains, and RL algorithms (PPO and GRPO) we measured, the scaling exponents order as environment $>$ task $>$ trajectory. When environment diversity is decomposed into world and environment-structure diversity, its advantage is mainly driven by the former, while the latter is just comparable to the task diversity. Second, we use the fitted laws as a common ruler to price synthesis methods, capturing each method's efficiency as a per-axis discount factor and identifying critical synthetic-to-real ratios at which collapse begins; these ratios differ by an order of magnitude across axes. Third, we cast budget allocation as a constrained optimization under our laws and discounts, deriving a Chinchilla-style compute-optimal recipe and validating it at held-out scale. The empirical results demonstrate that following the recipe yields up to $1.7\times$ compute efficiency over the dominant ``scale-the-trajectories'' practice.

</details>

Abstract: Physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) are trained to minimize the residual of a prescribed equation, so a low training loss does not indicate whether a coefficient in that equation was specified incorrectly. This study evaluates what can be inferred about the encoded parameter from the trained network alone. When the residual is linear in the parameter, written as $A - νB$, the least-squares estimate $\hatν = A^{\top}B/B^{\top}B$ is obtained from a single evaluation of the frozen network, without retraining or reference data. Its accuracy depends on how closely the network satisfies its own equation, quantified by a normalized residual $ρ$. Recovery error is associated with $ρ$ (log-log correlation 0.72 across 36 convection-diffusion networks), and empirical exponents of 0.63 to 1.20 across three equations decompose into one half plus a measurable angular term. For Burgers' equation, a single-model test based on a block-bootstrap standard error flagged 3 of 300 correctly specified networks (observed false-alarm rate 1%), all 2% misspecifications at a practical budget, and two thirds of 1% misspecifications after convergence. For the self-similar blowup rate of the Córdoba-Córdoba-Fontelos equation, where the rate also enters the architecture, the estimator recovers about half of the misspecification, whereas $ρ$ increases approximately quadratically and distinguishes a 0.2% error. When the incorrect rate enters both the equation and the architecture, errors of up to 2% were not detected at the precision reached. At practical budgets, the raw loss can be lower for the misspecified model, a scaling effect that $ρ$ does not share.

</details>

Abstract: Collaborative inference serves as a critical methodology in edge intelligence. Existing collaborative inference methods operate by splitting the deep neural network (DNN) model between edge devices and cloud servers. However, these methods need to load different models for multiple split points when the communication or device changes, thus resulting in long switching times and substantial storage overhead on the edge side. To address this issue, we propose a Dynamic Split Network (DS-Net), which not only enables efficient split point switching but also enhances the collaborative speed and accuracy across various scenarios. Specifically, we design a dynamic switching framework that enables flexible selection among multiple split points within a single model, thereby reducing storage, memory, and switching overhead. To mitigate the feature distribution shifts and task-relevant information loss caused by dynamic splitting and high-ratio compression, we further propose a hierarchical compensation mechanism. Local alignment calibrates the decoded feature distribution at each split point, while task-specific global compensation restores task-relevant information at the global level. In addition, we incorporate sample-adaptive global compensation selection into split-point selection based on sample complexity and runtime resource conditions. Experimental results demonstrate that DS-Net reduces memory overhead by 74.6% and achieves millisecond-level switching latency. Moreover, under a 10 Mbps bandwidth constraint on the Nano platform, DS-Net outperforms state-of-the-art collaborative methods by achieving 1.2% higher accuracy on the COCO dataset, reducing latency by 42.5%, and improving throughput by a factor of 1.8$\times$.

</details>

Abstract: Line-parallel language models advance multiple lines concurrently for substantial speedups. However, frameworks like the Line-Coupled Language Model (LCLM) enforce a fixed commitment schedule across lines. Because dependencies vary within text, a fixed schedule cannot hold back a line whose prerequisites remain uncomputed, forcing premature commitments and cascading reasoning errors. To address this limitation, we introduce the Lattice Language Model (LatLM), making commitment schedules an adaptive decoding-time choice. Text occupies a 2D lattice where eligible frontier tokens are scored each round; a policy commits confident candidates while deferring unready ones for extended context. One forward pass supervises entire sampled trajectories via 2D causal masking, while an append-only cache retains exact states. At 482M parameters, we establish two findings. (1) LatLM matches a data- and capacity-matched autoregressive model on held-out text when decoded sequentially, while its adaptive parallel decoder achieves near-autoregressive accuracy on independent-line tasks using 25--41% as many decoding rounds. (2) When cross-line dependencies cause LCLM's fixed schedule to fail, deferred commitment substantially improves accuracy while retaining parallelism where dependencies permit. On mixed dependency chains, LatLM achieves 96.5% accuracy versus LCLM's 78.5%, using fewer than half the decoding rounds of the autoregressive baseline.

</details>

Abstract: Clinical multimodal models can learn enrollment shortcuts shared across modalities and fail on new cohorts. Attention-based probing also reveals substantially more useful information in the frozen representation than linear probing, indicating that conventional linear evaluation may underestimate representation quality. In addition, the benefits of pretraining become more pronounced with increasing encoder capacity. Overall, the proposed framework demonstrates that physics-aware, multi-domain self-supervised pretraining can improve label efficiency, robustness, and transferability across diverse Raman spectroscopy tasks.
